**2026-07-11 update 17 (twenty-second round: mainshock focal mechanism from GlobalCMT -- a well-powered null, including on the M6+ target it was built for).** With rounds 19-21 converging on "the next gain needs information the catalogue does not carry," the mainshock source geometry (faulting style) was tested as exactly such a channel -- distinct from magnitude and location and from the round-18 static Slab2 position, since a subduction-interface thrust and a crustal strike-slip of equal size can escalate differently, and the mechanism is known at forecast time (routine within hours) so no label leak is possible. The full 1996-2024 GlobalCMT catalogue was assembled with complete temporal coverage (the 1976-2020 combined file plus reviewed monthly 2021-2024 plus the quick catalogue), yielding 5,648 Japan-box solutions, and each mainshock was matched to its moment tensor by the stable catalogue index -- 92.5% of the M6+ mainshocks matched, near-complete on the rare-event stratum that matters. Seven nodal-plane-ambiguity-free focal features (the P/T/B principal-axis plunges plus the rake sine/cosine and both dips) were appended to the confirmed stack, with the reference verified bit-identical to the round-21 baseline so the delta isolates the mechanism, and the variant demonstrably enters the trees rather than being a degenerate no-op. It changes nothing: pooled all-forecast +0.000 (CI [-0.004,+0.004]), M6+ follow-up -0.001, M6+ mainshock -0.015 (n.s.), Taiwan-excluded follow-up +0.003 -- a well-powered null on every stratum, reproduced on an independent second pooling. The most plausible reading is that the model already infers the tectonic setting from location and depth, so the explicit mechanism is redundant information under a new label -- the same saturation seen when a learned encoder, a cross-region catalogue, and a global-scale catalogue each added nothing. This is now the fourth independent line pointing to the same diagnosis; recorded as a lever tried and found null on its target, not a ceiling, and the search continues for a channel carrying information that location and the catalogue genuinely lack, or for a structurally different target.

**2026-07-11 update 18 (twenty-third round: sub-Mc micro-seismicity / catalogue completeness -- the gain saturates at completeness ~1.0 and the template-matching ultra-tail is null-to-negative, a fifth independent saturation line).** With rounds 19-22 converging on "the next gain needs information the catalogue does not carry," one untested class of genuinely new information is catalogue COMPLETENESS itself: the dense sub-Mc micro-seismicity a template-matching catalogue resolves but a standard-completeness catalogue misses, concentrated in the immediate post-mainshock coda where nucleation signal would live. Japan sub-Mc coverage is registration-gated, so the clean open test was run cross-region on the Southern California QTM catalogue (Ross et al. 2019; 1.81 million events, completeness ~0.3, 2008-2017). The design is a single-catalogue, multi-completeness contrast that eliminates every cross-catalogue artefact: independent Mm>=3.0 mainshocks (Gardner-Knopoff) with an escalation label defined on events at least a magnitude unit below the mainshock (>=2.0, far above every truncation, so labels are bit-identical across variants), and strict-causal density features (first 6 h, aftershock count/rate/b-value/inter-event and spatial statistics) rebuilt three times from the SAME catalogue truncated at completeness 1.7 (standard proxy), 1.0 (this project's pipeline level) and 0.3 (full). The leak-free primary (6 h features only; kept sequences exclude escalations already realised within 6 h, machine-verified to contain zero label events inside the window) shows skill rise from 1.7 to 1.0 -- +0.031 AUC at the 7-day horizon (CI [+0.006,+0.057], P 0.99) -- then FALL from 1.0 to 0.3: the ultra-tail change is -0.026 (CI [-0.052,-0.001], P 0.02), a statistically resolved non-monotonicity rather than an underpowered null, and specific to the short horizon (at 30 days the two are indistinguishable), consistent with short-term aftershock incompleteness corrupting the sub-1.0 events in the decisive early coda. The optimum completeness is ~1.0 -- exactly the level this project's Japan pipeline already builds features at -- so deeper catalogue density is, in isolation, not a route to higher skill; the one caveat left open by adversarial review is a sub-completeness by ETAS-context interaction, which a density-only isolation cannot test. Recorded as a lever tried and found null-to-negative below completeness 1.0 on target, not a ceiling: a fifth independent line -- learned encoder, cross-region, global-scale, source geometry, and now catalogue density -- pointing to the same saturation of the location-plus-catalogue paradigm under this target, and the search continues for a channel carrying information the catalogue genuinely lacks or a structurally different target.

**2026-07-12 update 19 (twenty-fourth round: crustal-specialist, migration-conditioned escalation -- the one channel with an established real signal, and it is real-but-redundant, a sixth independent saturation line).** Five converging null lines said the next gain needs information location and the catalogue do not carry; the one arc that DID find a physically real signal is foreshock radial-migration (foreshocks contracting toward the mainshock nucleus over ~14 days, crustal-specific, with subduction megathrust statistically separating out), so the natural test was whether that signal TRANSFERS to escalation forecasting. The population was restricted to crustal mainshocks (depth <= 30 km) and six pre-mainshock migration-kinematics features were added to the confirmed stack -- the Spearman distance-versus-time trend (negative = contraction), the linear shrink rate and its R-squared, the early-half-versus-late-half centroid approach, the azimuthal concentration, and the log foreshock count -- all computed strictly before the trigger time, within a magnitude-only Gardner-Knopoff radius, and relative to the KNOWN trigger location, so the future strong-follow-up hypocentre is never used (leak-safety confirmed by adversarial review, which bit-reproduced the per-fold features from scratch). Across the 13-fold 1996-2024 walk-forward it changes nothing: crustal all-forecast +0.003 (CI [-0.003,+0.009], P 0.83), every M6+ stratum slightly negative but not significant (n=28-49, P 0.06-0.11 = noise, not harm). The reference bit-reproduces the established full-set baseline (0.753, 1151 positives), and the null is robust to the training set -- training on all depths and evaluating on the crustal subset gives +0.001. A univariate diagnostic explains it precisely: the migration geometry IS directionally correct (escalating crustal sequences contract more -- radial-trend mean -0.138 versus -0.094) but weak (single-feature AUC 0.52-0.55), while the foreshock COUNT is strong (AUC 0.694) and already held by the baseline; the geometry therefore adds zero incremental skill. Crustal restriction alone also does not help (0.734 < 0.753). This is real-but-redundant: the only lever in the whole arc with an established real signal contributes nothing beyond what the count features already carry -- a sixth independent saturation line (learned encoder, cross-region, global-scale, source geometry, catalogue density, and now migration geometry) of the location-plus-catalogue paradigm under this target. Recorded as a lever tried and found redundant, not a ceiling; the one residual left open is a SoCal-QTM crustal augmentation of the migration model (a redundancy-driven null makes rescue unlikely but conditional/non-linear value with more positives is not formally excluded), and the search continues for information the catalogue genuinely lacks or a structurally different target.

**2026-07-13 update 20 (target redefinition: a literature reverse-engineering of alternative forecast targets, and the aleatory-ceiling diagnosis that motivates a ground-motion exceedance target).** Six independent saturation lines (rounds 19-24) established that the location-plus-catalogue paradigm is signal-saturated under the current sequence-escalation target, so the next structural lever is the target definition itself. An adversarially-verified literature reverse-engineering (multi-source, 22 confirmed claims) mapped the alternative-target space and closed two candidates. Spatial aftershock-nucleation forecasting, whose published state of the art (DeVries et al. 2018, AUC 0.85) is reproduced exactly by a two-parameter logistic regression on distance and slip (Mignan and Broccardo 2019), is a distance-driven easy-negative artifact that a hard-negative-controlled version would score below, not above. The absolute-magnitude / strong-subsequent target (NESTORE-family) is structurally identical to this project's current escalation label and is statistically power-starved in Japan (single-digit positive clusters), its headline 0.94 accuracy being a class-imbalance majority baseline rather than an AUC. The literature's own top recommendation, the Gulia and Wiemer (2019) b-value foreshock-traffic-light, is already implemented in this pipeline (features_ftls.py, the published early-versus-background b comparison) and contributes +0.001 to the forecast-only AUC, i.e. already null, corroborated by this project's own split-half demonstration that the apparent b-drop is an incompleteness artifact. The decisive diagnosis is that the current target's ~0.75 ceiling is aleatory-dominated rather than epistemic (Lombardi 2017: ETAS parametric uncertainty is far smaller than the process's aleatory variability, so whether a given sequence escalates is a near-memoryless draw once the rate is known), which is exactly why the theoretically-optimal ETAS forward-forecast feature saturates there. The structural response is to redefine the target onto a quantity whose signal is deterministic-physics-dominated rather than aleatory-tail-dominated: a prospective binary forecast of short-term instrumental-intensity exceedance at strong-motion stations within an active sequence, with the threshold placed in the BODY of the intensity distribution (cohort base rate ~20%) so the label is driven by the ETAS-predictable moderate-event rate rather than a single rare-event tail, and with a deterministic magnitude-to-intensity (GMPE + Vs30 site) layer adding an observable, spatially-deterministic prediction axis absent from the purely-temporal escalation label. Hard-negatives are non-exceeding neighbour stations matched on static distance and site so the model cannot solve it by geometry alone, and genuine skill is measured as delta-AUC over a static GMPE/climatology baseline with time- and space-permutation controls -- the pilot gate that must pass before commitment, since the raw AUC will trivially reach 0.90 on the deterministic physics and only the time-varying sequence-state increment counts. Feasibility confirmed: the JMA seismic-intensity database exposes an open, registration-free JSON API (per-station observed intensity for all felt earthquakes), station-window positives on the order of 10^3-10^4 dissolve the small-sample constraint, and the honest-evaluation and walk-forward machinery carries over unchanged. Recorded as the target-redefinition step of the arc -- a planned pivot with its first-gate experiment defined, not a result -- consistent with the standing rule that a high AUC explained by trivial geometry is hollow and only genuine time-varying skill counts.

**2026-07-14 update 21 (ground-motion exceedance -- the pivot's first gate: the genuine time-varying increment is null, so the ground-motion target is a tried-and-missed lever, not a route to 0.90).** The redefined target from update 20 was taken to its gate. Per-station JMA instrumental-intensity exceedance was assembled from the open intensity API for 178 Mm>=6.0 sequences (162,125 station-intensity records, epoch 2011): each station felt in the first 6 h of a sequence is asked whether its shaking in the (6 h, 7 d] forecast window exceeds a threshold, and genuine skill is the increment of the evolving 6 h aftershock-field features over a static geometry baseline (distance-to-mainshock, magnitude, depth, position) in honest temporal walk-forward. On the raw exceedance label the absolute AUC trivially reaches 0.93-0.96 but it is entirely static geometry -- the dynamic increment is -0.006, null -- exactly the hollow-AUC failure mode the standing rule warns about. To make the label itself geometry-free the target was residualised four ways (a mainshock-GMPE residual; a train-fit and a cross-fit HistGBT de-trend of the expected intensity; and a productive-subset restriction), across exceedance thresholds delta 0/0.5/1.0. The genuine dynamic-over-static increment is null in every one. Powered up with an expanding-window blocked-temporal design pooling 88 test sequences and 24,034 station-windows, the increment at the balanced threshold is -0.0004 (CI [-0.065,+0.065]) with static and dynamic models both at AUC ~0.48 on the geometry-free label; an earlier +0.088 on a single 34-sequence split was confirmed to be low-power noise. The adversarial reviewer's steelman -- that genuine skill hides in swarm-like, aftershock-productive sequences where no single dominant source fixes the geometry and is diluted in the pool -- was tested by retraining the whole gate on the high-productivity subset alone, and it too is null (increment -0.023, CI [-0.093,+0.034]). The genuine-label AUC across the family is ~0.48-0.69, an order of magnitude short of 0.90, and no formulation lifts the time-varying increment above zero. Recorded as a lever tried and found null: the ground-motion exceedance target's high absolute AUC is deterministic near-field geometry (the same reject-mode as the 0.5-degree spatial hollow AUC and DeVries 2018), it carries no genuine time-varying forecast increment, and the search continues for a structurally different target -- the remaining unbenchmarked candidates (escalation timing, Bath-law deviation) tested next on the existing catalogue rather than dismissed as aleatory. Not a ceiling claim.

**2026-07-14 update 22 (the two remaining target-redefinition candidates -- escalation timing and Bath-law deviation -- both resolve to null, the Bath apparent-positive caught as a cross-sequence overlap artifact by verifying the reviewer's own clearance rather than trusting it).** With ground-motion closed (update 21), the deep-research map left two unbenchmarked alternative targets, tested here on the existing catalogue rather than dismissed as aleatory. Escalation TIMING -- among sequences that do escalate, whether the strong follow-up onset is imminent (<= tau days) or delayed, from the leak-safe 6 h aftershock field -- is null at every threshold (tau 0.5/1/2 d, pooled blocked-temporal, dynamic-over-static increment +0.02 to +0.045, every CI spanning zero, all AUC ~0.5-0.6): escalation onset is an Omori-memoryless draw that the observed state does not sharpen. Bath-law DEVIATION -- forecasting the size of the eventual largest aftershock (Dm = Mm - largest forecast-window aftershock, Bath ~ 1.2) from the 6 h field -- first looked positive: a +0.077 dynamic-over-static increment (AUC 0.60 to 0.68, N=616) that survived a train-only-median leak-guard, an ablation dropping the early-largest-aftershock feature, and a row-permutation null, and that an adversarial review reproduced and cleared as a genuine ETAS-productivity signal. Verifying that clearance rather than trusting it, a strict-independence filter that drops every sequence whose space-time window contains another sequence's mainshock (212 of 616) collapses the increment to -0.023 (CI [-0.065,+0.018]) on the 404 contamination-free sequences: the entire +0.077 came from overlapping sequences where the 6 h field and the forecast-window largest aftershock share events from a different nearby cluster -- shared-event contamination, not forecast skill, a within-block row-level artifact that both the block-boundary reasoning and the global permutation-null miss, the same failure family as the retracted joint-fusion leaks. Both candidates recorded null. Taken with the six catalogue-saturation lines and the ground-motion residual, the catalogue-derived target-redefinition space is now mapped: every reformulation reduces to the same genuine-but-aleatory-capped ~0.75 ETAS-context/productivity signal or is null. Not a ceiling claim -- it records that redefining the target on the catalogue alone does not open a genuine 0.90 route, and the remaining structural levers are a new observation system that densifies or adds a non-catalogue channel (template-matched microseismicity in the first 6 h; near-fault real-time strain or GNSS) or the untested GPU-scale sequence-transformer regime, which the search turns to next.

**2026-07-14 update 23 (next structural lever after the catalogue target-space is mapped: a self-supervised catalogue foundation model -- launched, pilot in progress, not yet a result).** Updates 18-22 established that every catalogue-derived target reduces to the same genuine-but-aleatory-capped ~0.75 ETAS signal or is null, so the next lever is not another target but a different way of extracting the catalogue: a learned representation that could capture DEPARTURES from ETAS (anisotropic triggering, fault-structure-dependent decay, foreshock-specific patterns) which the parametric point-process model, near-optimal for its own form, cannot. Round 19's small supervised two-stream GRU (AUC 0.722, below the 0.753 hand-feature stack) already probed this and under-performed, but its ceiling was the tiny labelled set (1097 sequences); the untested regime is self-supervised PRETRAINING on the full catalogue, where labels are not the bottleneck. The corpus is 2.45M JMA events (867k at M>=2) tokenised into 36,115 fixed-length event-sequence windows, over which a small transformer encoder is pretrained by masked-event reconstruction (no escalation labels), then frozen and used to embed each sequence's first-6h stream; the pilot gate is whether that embedding, added to the confirmed ETAS-context stack, lifts the pooled all-forecast AUC above the established ~0.753 in honest walk-forward. Recorded honestly as a launched experiment with its gate defined, not a result -- and with the standing caveat that if the catalogue's information is genuinely aleatory-capped, even a foundation model returns ~0.75, which would itself be an informative closure pointing the search decisively to non-catalogue observation channels. The pilot runs on CPU as a lower bound on representation learning; a positive signal would justify scaling on GPU, a null a decisive record that catalogue representation does not open the 0.90 route. Not a ceiling claim -- an experiment in flight.

**2026-07-15 update 24 (the catalogue foundation model, frozen-embedding pilot: null -- the pretrained representation is redundant with the ETAS stack, a lower-bound closure that points to non-catalogue channels).** The step-3 gate from update 23 was run. Each escalation sequence's first-6h in-disc event stream was rebuilt in the encoder's own 5-feature window-relative format, standardised with the pretraining-corpus statistics, padded to length 64, passed through the frozen masked-event-pretrained encoder (best validation reconstruction 0.7137), and mean-pooled to a 96-dimensional embedding; a HistGBT was then fit on the confirmed ETAS-context stack with versus without the embedding in paired eleven-fold walk-forward (the two folds whose build directories had been reclaimed by disk cleanup were dropped, leaving a paired identical-fold comparison so the delta is unbiased, and the no-embedding arm reproduced the saved fold scores bit-for-bit on all eleven folds, confirming the reproduction). The increment is null: the raw 96-dimensional embedding gives -0.0063 (a tree-split dilution of the 40-feature stack, since the compact train-fit PCA-8 form lands at -0.0008 with a confidence interval spanning zero, an exact null), the embedding alone scores AUC 0.544 (barely above chance and fully redundant with the ETAS features once combined), and the M6+ layers are null. This is the seventh convergent saturation and mirrors round 19 exactly: both a from-scratch supervised sequence model and a frozen self-supervised representation converge to redundant-with-ETAS, so the catalogue event sequence -- however it is encoded -- carries no short-term escalation signal beyond ETAS in this Japanese data. Recorded as a tried-and-missed lever, not a ceiling: the pilot closes the cheap frozen-embedding bet and is a lower bound on representation learning (a full unfrozen end-to-end fine-tune at larger GPU scale is a distinct untested regime that the seven convergent nulls weigh against but do not disprove), and the informative closure is that catalogue representation does not open the 0.90 route, pointing the search to non-catalogue observation channels. Not a ceiling claim.

**2026-07-15 update 25 (the foundation model's unfrozen end-to-end fine-tune, at the current scale and at a larger model+corpus scale, is also null -- the catalogue-representation-scaling direction is closed).** The frozen-embedding null (update 24) left two objections: a frozen encoder cannot adapt to the task, and only the small RPi5-pretrained encoder was tested. Both were closed on free-tier Kaggle GPU (no paid compute). The pretrained encoder was unfrozen and trained end-to-end on the escalation label -- its first-6h stream embedding concatenated with the confirmed ETAS stack through an MLP head, per fold on year<split with a leak-free temporal early-stop -- in two arms: the small d96/3L encoder, and a larger d192/6L/8H encoder pretrained on GPU on a four-times-larger corpus (188,890 windows from 1.51M M>=1.5 events, whose validation reconstruction of 0.68 versus the small encoder's 0.71 confirms it genuinely learned a better representation). Both are null: the fine-tuned network alone scores 0.73 (small) and 0.73 (big), below the confirmed GBT stack's 0.753, and the 50/50 rank-blend merely recovers the baseline (small +0.0036, CI [-0.0023, +0.0094], P 0.887; big +0.0002, CI [-0.0064, +0.0067], P 0.510). The M6+ layer point estimates are small-positive but non-significant (n=32, confidence intervals spanning zero, P around 0.82-0.87), consistent with the recurring small-sample-noise pattern and recorded as not a finding. So unfreezing does not help and neither does scaling the model and the pretraining corpus -- the better reconstruction of the bigger encoder does not translate into any escalation skill. Taken with the frozen pilot and round 19's from-scratch sequence model, the catalogue sequence representation adds nothing genuine over the ETAS stack whether frozen, fine-tuned, or scaled up, so the "scale would help" hypothesis is now tested and null and the foundation-model / catalogue-representation-scaling direction is closed. Not a ceiling claim -- a tried-and-missed lever; the genuine remaining information is a non-catalogue observation channel, which the search turns to next.

**2026-07-15 update 26 (non-catalogue observation channels: the two physical candidates exist but have neither an openly downloadable dense feed nor any published forecast skill, so the openly-executable lever set is for now mapped -- not a ceiling).** With the catalogue-representation direction closed (updates 18-25), the remaining genuine information is a non-catalogue observation channel. A multi-source, adversarially-verified review of the openly-accessible options for Japan examined the two physically-motivated candidates: continuous near-fault crustal strain and tilt (NIED Hi-net borehole tiltmeters, JMA Tokai volumetric strainmeters) and continuous groundwater level (the AIST/Geological Survey of Japan observation-well network). Both exist as coordinated instrument networks, but neither has any published demonstration of short-term forecast skill above an ETAS baseline under hard-negative control; the demonstrated state is uniformly retrospective, detection-capability, or hypothetical-preslip simulation, and the single strongest result is a hard null (NIED Hi-net borehole tiltmeters recorded no clear precursory tilt or preslip before the 2011 Mw9.0 Tohoku earthquake, corroborated by a 2024 re-analysis that also dissolves a competing GNSS preslip claim). A direct feasibility check then found no openly downloadable dense feed for either channel: the dense nationwide Hi-net tiltmeter channel is not exposed for programmatic download (only the seismic waveforms are), and the openly-licensed AIST/GSJ strain, tilt, and groundwater data is served through a plotting viewer with request-gated raw time series and is regionally sparse. So the two candidate channels are neither pre-validated as forecasters nor openly-and-densely accessible, and the openly-executable lever set is, for now, mapped. This is recorded as the current state of the open-data search, not a ceiling claim: the goal -- a genuine hard-negative-controlled short-term escalation AUC of 0.90 -- remains open and awaits a genuinely new openly-executable channel or method, while the operational 7-day and monthly ETAS forecasts continue to run.

**2026-07-15 update 27 (a new well-powered lever: surface-deformation escalation forecasting from open InSAR via COMET LiCSAR -- launched; feature extraction and the skill test are next).** With the catalogue-representation direction closed, the remaining genuine information is the surface deformation the catalogue discards, and the openly-accessible dense source for it over Japan is COMET LiCSAR: analysis-ready Sentinel-1 geocoded interferograms, coherence, and look-vector layers, downloadable without any gating from the open product tree (the NASA OPERA surface-displacement product does not yet cover Japan, so LiCSAR is used). A Japan frame index was built by crawling the product tree and filtering frame footprints to the Japan bounding box, yielding 66 frames across 22 Sentinel-1 relative-orbit tracks. Crucially, and unlike the earlier point-geodesy channels (offshore seafloor GNSS-A with 17 events, borehole pore pressure with 2), the escalation test on this channel is well-powered: of the independent M>=4.5 mainshocks in the Sentinel-1 era (2016 onward) that fall inside a Japan frame, 889 have surface-deformation coverage with 233 escalation-positive, and the crustal subset (depth <=30 km, where surface InSAR is most relevant) has 393 with 130 positive. The plan is to extract, for each sequence, the pre-mainshock line-of-sight surface deformation in the epicentral area -- velocity, recent acceleration, and the spatial gradient of displacement as a strain-rate proxy, with coherence and atmospheric masking and the look-vector conversion from line-of-sight to displacement components -- add it to the confirmed ETAS stack, and test in leak-free, hard-negative-controlled walk-forward whether it lifts the pooled all-forecast AUC above the established 0.753. No prior work has tested InSAR surface deformation as a short-term sequence-escalation forecaster on a hard-negative-controlled sample of this size. Recorded as a launched, well-powered experiment, with feature extraction and the skill test as the immediate next steps.

**2026-07-16 update 28 (InSAR lever, step 1: the true-footprint sample is 491 sequences / 108 escalation-positive — smaller than the update-27 bounding-box estimate but still well-powered; leak-safe feature extraction is running).** The update-27 power figures (889 sequences / 233 positive; crustal 393/130) were computed against frame *bounding boxes*. Testing each epicenter against the true frame footprint polygons (`metadata/<frame>-poly.txt`, point-in-polygon over all 66 Japan frames) reduces the covered sample to **491 sequences with 108 escalation-positive** (crustal depth ≤30 km: 145 with 40 positive): Sentinel-1 swaths are diagonal, so bounding boxes overcount, and offshore epicenters outside the imaged area drop out. This is still an order of magnitude more positives than any point-geodesy channel tested here (seafloor GNSS-A: 17 events; borehole pore pressure: 2). Extraction of the pre-mainshock signal is running: for each covered sequence, interferograms whose *both* acquisitions predate the mainshock by at least one day (strictly pre-event, hence known at forecast time — no leak), within a 150-day lookback and up to a 48-day temporal baseline, are pulled from the CEDA archive tree, and the epicentral line-of-sight displacement is measured as the median over a ±0.10° box referenced against a 0.15–0.35° ring (differencing out long-wavelength atmosphere; GACOS correction is a planned refinement), with the unwrapped-phase nodata mask applied and a least-squares plane fit supplying a spatial-gradient (strain-rate proxy) feature. The walk-forward harness (8 InSAR columns appended to the confirmed stack, joined by a fold-invariant physical event key) is staged and verified: the new fold script's reference model bit-reproduces the round-22 reference (max abs score diff 0.0), and the pre-registered primary endpoint is the pooled all-forecast delta within the covered cohort. One methodological note worth recording: the internal `ms_idx` event key is *not* invariant across fold directories built on different catalogue epochs (zero agreement between a cat-1990 and a cat-2000 fold), so the InSAR join uses the physical (time, latitude, longitude, magnitude) key instead; a re-check of the round-22 focal join, which used `ms_idx`, confirmed its lookup table contained both keyspaces and agreed on 6181/6213 common events, so the round-22 null stands.

**2026-07-17 update 29 (InSAR lever, result: the pre-registered covered-cohort test is null at current coverage — and the honest reading is *underpowered*, not closed; the extraction and harness are sound and the lever stays open at better coverage).** The extraction completed cleanly: 11,046 strictly pre-mainshock interferogram measurements over 345 of the 491 covered sequences (the rest fail the pixel-count quality thresholds or have no qualifying pair in the 150-day window). On the pre-registered primary endpoint — the pooled all-forecast AUC delta within the InSAR-covered cohort, both positives and negatives restricted to covered events — the eight-feature model changes nothing: delta −0.004 with a 95% bootstrap interval of [−0.027, +0.020] against the covered-cohort base of 0.746 (52 positives, 265 negatives). A signed-feature variant (retaining the line-of-sight sign of the primary-frame velocity and acceleration, which the absolute-value aggregation had discarded — an adversarial-review finding) is equally null (−0.005 [−0.028, +0.020]), and the crustal subset's positive point estimate (+0.02–0.04) sits inside a wide interval on 16 positives. The decisive caveat, raised by adversarial review and confirmed by direct recomputation, is *power*: only the 2018 and 2020 folds ever train a live InSAR model, and their training sets contain just 1.8% and 3.6% covered events, because Sentinel-1 coverage starts in 2016 and walk-forward training must precede the test window; the 2016 fold (21 of the 52 covered positives) has zero covered training events, so its variant is the reference by construction and mechanically freezes a quarter of the cohort at delta zero, halving the apparent interval width. Recomputed on the two live folds only, the honest headline is −0.002 [−0.035, +0.035] on 31 positives — an interval that cannot exclude effects of ±0.03 in either direction. Verification: no leak (both acquisitions strictly pre-event, median data-end five days before the mainshock), no masking bug (the augmented model genuinely moves essentially every covered score; the physical-key join is collision-free at 345/345), and every headline number was re-run twice bit-identically. Recorded as a lever *tried at current open-data coverage*: the binding constraint is that LiCSAR frames cover 491 of ~6,500 stack events, and the named next steps that would thicken the covered cohort are on-demand open Sentinel-1 processing (ASF HyP3) or the ARIA-S1-GUNW standard product for events outside LiCSAR frames, GACOS tropospheric correction plus coherence masking to cut per-pair noise, and a 2022/2024 re-split of the last fold so that later test years see the accumulated covered training data.

**2026-07-17 update 30 (InSAR lever, follow-through: the two coverage/noise levers named in update 29 are now running — a coherence-masked re-extraction of the LiCSAR cohort, and an on-demand burst-InSAR densification through ASF HyP3 — after a feasibility sweep that killed one option and right-sized another).** The feasibility facts, each established by direct query: the ARIA-S1-GUNW standard product has **zero** coverage over Japan (an ASF search API count over the Japan bounding box returns 0 products, against 26,323 for a Southern-California control box, so the null is real and that option is dead); the GACOS tropospheric-correction layers hosted alongside LiCSAR are access-restricted (HTTP 403) and are not used; the solid-earth-tide and ionosphere layers *are* open, but direct inspection shows they vary only on scales far larger than the ±0.35° analysis window — the ring reference already cancels them, so they were dropped as correction layers rather than cargo-culted in. What does bite at the analysis scale is decorrelation noise: the pair-level coherence rasters are open, and a sample pair has median coherence ≈0.04, meaning the unmasked epicentral medians admit many near-noise pixels. A full re-extraction of the 11,046-measurement LiCSAR set with coherence-masked variants (thresholds at coherence ≈0.12 and ≈0.24, plus inner/ring mean-coherence features) is running, with the unmasked channel verified bit-identical to the round-25 extraction on shared pairs. On the coverage side, ASF HyP3's on-demand processing (8,000 free credits/month; a single-burst interferogram costs 1 credit) was smoke-tested end-to-end — a pre-mainshock burst pair over the 2024 Noto epicenter processed in ~30 minutes and yielded a usable epicentral extraction (30,816 inner pixels from one burst, with coherence and look vectors included in the product) — and then right-sized honestly: a land-mask prefilter over the 1,504 LiCSAR-uncovered Sentinel-1-era sequences shows that only **64** of them (26 escalation-positive) have any land within the ±0.10° epicentral box; the rest are offshore epicenters that no land-surface InSAR can reach, a constraint that was applied *before* submission rather than discovered in wasted processing. All 580 planned burst pairs for those 64 sequences (same leak-safe selection discipline as round 25) were submitted in one batch and are being downloaded, extracted in their native UTM geometry, and appended to the feature store as they complete. The honest expected gain is bounded — covered escalation-positives rise from 108 to at most ~134 — so this closes out what land-reaching open InSAR can contribute at current design, while the offshore majority is recorded as requiring a structurally different observable (coastal-deformation boxes or seafloor geodesy) rather than being silently dropped.

**2026-07-18 update 31 (InSAR lever, coverage build-out complete: HyP3 burst densification finished, the catalog extended 2.5 years with the open JMA provisional hypocenter lists, 2024–2026 events mapped into LiCSAR frames, and round 28 pre-registered before any results).** The HyP3 burst batch from update 30 completed cleanly: all 580 jobs processed and extracted (58 sequences, all 26 escalation-positives among them), with an unbiased displacement distribution (median signed dmm ≈ 0.4 mm) but a single-burst noise floor ~7× the frame-scale LiCSAR one (median |dmm| ≈ 12 mm vs ≈ 1.7 mm) — so the feature store now carries an explicit source flag rather than silently mixing noise regimes. The larger structural step attacks the power constraint named in update 29 from the calendar side: the JMA *final* catalog ends at 2023, but the open daily provisional hypocenter lists turn out to carry the full network solution down to M≈0, and all 928 daily pages from 2024-01-01 to 2026-07-16 were fetched and parsed (325,018 events at M≥1 in the Japan box; zero missing days). Validation before use: frequency–magnitude continuity across the source boundary (completeness magnitude 1.2 vs 1.2–1.3, Gutenberg–Richter b at M≥2 0.661–0.674 vs 0.663–0.684 — no discontinuity), and the M6.5+ list reproduces the known real sequence of the period (Noto 2024 M7.6, Hyūga-nada 2024 M7.1, Noto 2024-11 M6.6, and the late-2025/2026 Sanriku-oki M7.5/M7.7/M7.2 cluster). Because the provisional pages are on a rolling retention (mid-2023 already returns 404), the fetched cache is the archival copy. The escalation stack rebuilt on the extended catalog adds 727 new sequences (7,440 total) while every pre-2024 row is bit-identical to the previous stack and the ETAS parameters are untouched (fitted through 2020, so any test window from 2020 onward remains leak-safe). Mapping the new 2024–2026 mainshocks into the true LiCSAR frame footprints yields 145 covered events with 41 escalation-positives — a +38% increase over the entire 2016–2024 covered-positive count, concentrated exactly where walk-forward training finally has years of covered data behind it — and a pre-flight probe against the cached interferogram listings confirms 110 of the 145 (31 of 41 positives) have qualifying pre-mainshock pairs (the 35 zero-pair events split between stale frames and acquisition-window gaps; the 3 with usable land were submitted as a 30-job HyP3 fallback batch, already extracted, while the offshore remainder is out of reach of any land-surface InSAR). Honest sizing also retired a tempting idea before it consumed effort: of the 544 still-uncovered Sentinel-1-era positives, 85% have no in-frame land within 0.5°, and only ~21 sit within a physically defensible 35 km of it, so a "nearest-coast box" is a small lever, not a structural one — the offshore mass still requires seafloor geodesy, and the named next candidate on the noise axis is time-series (LiCSBAS-style) processing of the same frames, to be judged against the coherence-mask results. Everything downstream is pre-registered and queued: a written pre-registration fixes the round-28 primary endpoint (live-fold pooled covered-cohort delta over the 2018/2020–22/2022–24/2024–26 folds) before any evaluation ran, the late-fold re-split of the wide 2020–2025 window is set up as its own pre-registered analysis, and the coherence-masked re-extraction plus the full evaluation chain run unattended — results, whatever they show, in the next update.

**2026-07-18 update 32 (InSAR lever, rounds 26/26-ls/28 results: underpowered null at doubled coverage — and the power constraint finally has a name: CEDA product availability; round 29 backfill launched the same day).** The unattended chain from update 31 ran to completion overnight and all three pre-registered evaluations are in. Round 26 (the full coherence-masked LiCSAR re-extraction, 8,204 pairs) reports its primary — the live-fold pooled covered-cohort delta — at −0.010 with CI [−0.056, +0.032] on 41 positives: statistically indistinguishable from the unmasked round-25 result, so per-pair coherence masking alone does not change the picture. The late-fold re-split (round 26-ls) puts the same cohort under a different fold boundary and lands at −0.006 [−0.055, +0.039] — the null is not a fold-boundary artifact. Round 28, the headline act — catalog extended through mid-2026 with the validated JMA provisional lists, 2024–26 mainshocks mapped into LiCSAR frames, evaluation re-run over 15 folds with the pre-registered primary read off unmodified — comes in at −0.011 [−0.053, +0.029] on 53 covered positives, with every regression gate green (the pre-2024 fold bit-matches the old build exactly; the feature join is exact: 51 extracted events, 51 covered test rows). One seductive side-signal earned a hard look and a hard downgrade: the "established strata" M6+ followup delta went positive with a CI excluding zero (+0.020 [+0.004, +0.039]) — but of its nine positives exactly one carries InSAR coverage (one of 43 in the 15-fold version; the M6+ mainshock stratum: zero of 47), so the movement is model-refit perturbation on rows the satellites never measured, not deformation signal. It is recorded prominently so that no future round mistakes it for a lever. The genuinely load-bearing discovery came from auditing why coverage undershot expectations: the extraction planned 1,943 pairs for the 2024+ in-frame events but only 608 records landed, with zero download or listing errors — the missing interferograms simply do not exist yet on CEDA, because the LiCSAR automated processing lags the satellites by months on many frames. Net effect: 109 of the 145 mapped 2024–26 events (31 of 41 positives) contributed nothing, and round 28 ran at roughly half its achievable coverage. That reframes the constraint precisely — not sensor noise (masking null), not the calendar (catalog extended), but product availability plus offshore epicenters — and availability is attackable today: 31 of the 109 (10 positives, 8 of them crustal) have workable land at the epicenter, and round 29 was pre-registered and launched within hours — on-demand HyP3 burst interferometry for all 31, 310 jobs submitted against the free 8,000-credit monthly allowance, the same selection discipline as every round before it, evaluation queued unattended into fresh directories with the round-28 numbers as the locked comparison point. The remaining 78 (21 positives) sit with epicenters over water — that mass stays out of reach of any land-surface technique and keeps pointing, as it has since the coastal-box sizing, at seafloor geodesy. Two levers stay open regardless of round 29's outcome: LiCSBAS-style time-series processing on the covered frames (the noise axis), and periodic cost-free re-runs of the extraction as the CEDA backlog clears (the coverage axis — the pipeline is resume-safe by construction). Results of round 29, whatever they show, in the next update.

**2026-07-18 update 33 (round 29 same-day: HyP3 backfill lands all 31 targets, pre-registered primary stays null at pos=56 — and two conventions fell under audit: the coastal-box retirement is reversed at footprint scale, and the "test in Japan" assumption itself is now on the table).** The availability shortfall named in update 32 was closed the same afternoon for every event that the current feature definition can reach: 310 on-demand HyP3 burst jobs (ten pre-mainshock pairs per event, both acquisitions at least a day before the mainshock by construction of the search window), all 310 processed and extracted, all 31 target events landing in the feature store — fold-2024 coverage rose from 51 to 82 events with every earlier fold bit-frozen, and the pre-2024 regression gate stayed green. The pre-registered primary — the live-fold pooled covered-cohort delta — moves from −0.011 to −0.007 with CI [−0.046, +0.031] on 56 positives: a null, marginally narrower, recorded as-is. One accounting lesson worth its line: the backfill added ten covered label-positives but only three enter the forecast-only universe (seven had their qualifying event inside the six-hour observation window and are excluded by design), so coverage sizings must count forecast-only positives, not label positives. The covered-crustal stratum, for the third consecutive round, shows a positive point estimate (+0.022, CI spanning zero on 28 positives) — noted, not claimed. The more consequential movement came from a challenge to the framing itself. Asked, in effect, whether "the epicenter is under the sea" was physics or habit, the answer turned out to be habit: the epicentral-box feature definition requires land at the epicenter, but a preparatory slip patch deforms land tens of kilometres away (Boso-offshore slow-slip events are routinely seen by onshore GNSS). A point-source re-sizing (u ~ M0/4πμR²) over all 598 uncovered Sentinel-1-era positives — only 68 of which lack any land within 1.5° — finds that under a Mw 6 preparatory-slip scenario, 326 of them would exceed a 20-pair stacked LiCSAR noise floor on their nearest land, 76 even a single-interferogram floor. [Both of those floors were assumed rather than measured, and are superseded by update 52: the measured 20-pair floor is 7.7 mm, not 0.4 mm, and the count above it is 8, not 326.] The earlier coastal-box retirement, which counted distance to in-frame land instead of expected amplitude against the floor, is therefore formally corrected in the results file: the coastal/footprint direction is reopened as a structural lever, with honest caveats attached (the scenario band Mw 5.5–6 is null in GNSS point sensors; InSAR stacking is a spatially dense, different noise regime; a reachability sizing is not evidence a signal exists). The same audit surfaced four more convention-bound closures — the fixed inner-box/ring scale that may subtract broad deep deformation along with the atmosphere, the 48-day maximum pair separation that blinds the features to slowly accumulating displacement, tide-gauge differencing as an untouched open coastal vertical-motion network, and the 1974-vintage Gardner–Knopoff windowing baked into sequence association. The largest of all: nothing requires this hypothesis to be tested in Japan, whose geography puts most epicenters over water. The global sequence stack built for the round-21 augmentation work already holds 2,629 Sentinel-1-era sequences with 762 escalation positives worldwide, LiCSAR's frame directory is global and open, and a frame-map build plus global event mapping is running now — if land-rich seismic belts (Anatolia, Iran, California, Chile) deliver the expected several-fold covered-positive count, the InSAR-escalation hypothesis gets its first properly powered test, with Japan as the transfer target rather than the test bench. Results in the next update.

**2026-07-18 update 34 (round 30 armed the same day: the global covered-cohort test is designed, pre-registered, and extracting — 2,624 LiCSAR frame footprints mapped worldwide, 814 covered sequences with 219 escalation positives, 662 of them with real pre-mainshock interferogram pairs).** The "test in Japan" audit item from update 33 went from idea to running pipeline within the afternoon. The frame map first: all 175 Sentinel-1 tracks enumerated through the same open JASMIN directory that serves the Japan frames, 2,656 frames listed and 2,624 footprint polygons cached (the one crash along the way — some frames publish literal "None" rows in their polygon files — was guarded and the build resumed from cache in seconds). Mapping the global sequence stack (the round-21 asset: ComCat Mm≥5.5 mainshocks worldwide, Japan box excluded at construction so the two cohorts never overlap) into those footprints gives 814 frame-covered Sentinel-1-era sequences carrying 219 escalation positives — four times Japan's covered-positive count — and the crustal (≤30 km) slice alone holds 429 sequences with 178 positives, more than six times the Japanese stratum that has shown a positive-but-null point estimate three rounds running. The pair pre-flight (the lesson of update 32: listings are not products) confirms 662 of the 814 events have at least one selectable strictly-pre-mainshock pair, with a median of 42 pairs per event; the 152 zero-pair events are recorded, 82 of them land-viable as a later HyP3 fallback batch. Extraction of the ~13k-pair workload is running unattended (747 frames, the identical selection and coherence-channel discipline as every round since 26, two to three days at Raspberry-Pi pace), with the full evaluation chained behind it. The harness is built and smoke-tested: the global stack carries the NESTORE/dynamics/FTLS feature families but no ETAS forward rates, so the pre-registration explicitly scopes any positive delta to "adds skill over a seismicity-feature baseline without explicit ETAS" and commits a Japan-transfer check before any stronger claim — while a null needs no such caveat. One trap was caught and fixed before results rather than after: the smoke run showed the 2016 fold trains on years with zero InSAR coverage, which forces its variant to equal its reference and would have entered every covered test row of that fold into the pooled cohort frozen at delta zero — the same dilution that round 25's review caught in Japan. The pre-registered primary therefore pools only the four folds whose training windows contain covered data, and the five-fold figure is demoted to a labeled diagnostic. Everything about this round runs on the free tier: open directory listings, open polygons, open interferograms, and a sequence stack that already existed. Results — the first properly powered test of whether pre-mainshock surface deformation carries escalation information, on geography chosen for the question instead of inherited from the project's origin — in the next update.

**2026-07-19 update 35 (while the global extraction runs: the long-baseline audit item closed with a calibration lesson, the short-pair integration lever tested and cleanly null, and the Japan ETAS lever declined to replicate on the global catalog).** Three threads landed while round 30's 13k-pair extraction grinds on. First, audit item three — "does anything require interferogram pairs to span at most 48 days?" — turned into a small odyssey with a generalizable ending. The LiCSAR index lists 60-366-day pairs as a fifth of the Japan archive, but every sampled one is an empty directory (0 of 36 products exist): the listings-are-not-products lesson of update 32, now confirmed at archive scale. Self-processing on HyP3 kept the route alive, and a three-stage smoke — best-coherence urban sites, then median-coherence vegetated sites, then, by accident of epicenter geometry, three sites whose epicentral discs are open ocean — produced the round's real finding: the pure-water discs, where true coherence is physically zero, all measure 73.9-74.3 on the processor's 0-255 scale. That is the estimator's noise floor, measured directly, and it re-reads every earlier number: the urban sites (172-243) are genuinely coherent at six-month baselines, but the vegetated median sites that had looked like "no temporal decay" (77-92, ratios 0.90-1.01) are actually sitting within a few counts of the floor, two of them collapsing onto it at 150+ days — the apparent stability was floor compression, and the pre-registered usability criterion, whose threshold sat below the floor, would have passed pure seawater. Long-baseline interferometry survives only in the top coherence decile; calibrate any coherence scale against open water before believing it. Second: the constructive alternative — integrate the short pairs we already extracted into accumulated displacement, a least-squares epoch inversion per event-frame (a scalar LiCSBAS-lite; 478 events, median ten epochs spanning 96 days, 3 mm network misclosure) — went through the full pre-registered harness against the strongest available reference, the stack plus all existing per-pair InSAR features. Result: −0.021, CI [−0.055, +0.011] on 55 covered positives; every fold slightly negative. The review's one substantive demand was the right one: with integration features present on only 2-6% of training rows, prove the model could have learned anything at that coverage. A synthetic informative feature injected into exactly the same covered rows (univariate AUC 0.80) came back at +0.090, CI [+0.027, +0.154] — the harness learns fine at this coverage, so the integration null is a clean "no signal", not "couldn't look". A seemingly significant M6+ stratum (+0.043) was dismissed the same way as round 29's: zero of its nine positives have integration coverage — refit noise on NaN rows, a trap now caught twice by the same one-line count. Third: the five global ETAS fits finished and the replication verdict is in — the +0.044 ETAS lever that reproduces in 13 of 13 Japanese folds shows +0.016 with CI [−0.008, +0.041] on the global ComCat stack, point-positive but not established, with the fit calibration itself mediocre (2.6x underprediction of M5.5+ counts against a catalog whose depth and completeness vary by subduction zone). Recorded as dilution at global catalog quality, not as evidence against the Japanese lever; the fits' real value is that the round-30 test can now be re-run against an ETAS-augmented strong baseline, closing the weak-baseline reservation the pre-registration flagged. One more piece of round-30 hygiene, established before results instead of after: a silent-404 audit found 38% of the planned global pairs are permanently absent from the archive (2015-2023 epochs uniformly, not a backlog), the extractor marks them done without logging a failure, and the achieved event-level coverage among fully-processed frames is running near half of the pre-flight expectation — so the readout gate now requires counting events-with-records against the 662/190 expectation, and the recovery lever (targeted HyP3 processing for archive-lost positives, a few hundred credits) is costed and waiting on the primary result. Nulls on two more conventions, a measured noise floor, a validated harness, and a strong baseline in hand — the properly powered global answer is still the next update.

**2026-07-20 update 36 (while the global InSAR extraction finishes, a directed literature sweep and two same-day pilots map the catalogue-plus-observation family and locate empirically why short-term escalation skill sits near 0.75 — a magnitude-draw decoupling, not a channel that was missed).** With the dense-InSAR rounds converging and the target a genuine 0.90, the question turned to whether any untested dense observation channel, or a more-deterministic target, could carry escalation information the catalogue discards. Four independent literature agents, each briefed with the full null history and told to be adversarial about leakage, converged on NO-GO for every dense channel: ambient-noise dv/v (pre-seismic claims are small-N, active-source, or foreshock-ray contamination of the seismicity signal already captured, with seasonal noise at the same amplitude as any claimed precursor), continuous-waveform deep learning used only to densify a catalogue (redundant with this project's sub-Mc QTM null), DAS (Japan coverage sparse, short, proprietary; zero forecasting studies), high-rate GNSS and short-term slow-slip detection (1 Hz is noisier than daily solutions for slow signals, and slow-slip precedes a minority of strong events while occurring constantly without one), and open borehole strain/tilt, groundwater, radon and geoelectric (below detection for M<7, or in the discredited-precursor category of the 2011 ICEF review). No method in the literature demonstrates hard-negative-controlled seven-day ROC AUC above 0.85 that survives temporal walk-forward: every published high score forecasts spatial aftershock pattern (a distance artefact — DeVries 2018 reproduced by a two-parameter logistic in Mignan and Broccardo 2019), or is retrospective, or laboratory, and the ten-year CSEP California and Italy OEF experiments confirm ETAS as the prospective frontier that nothing credibly beats — placing this project's walk-forward 0.75 with an internal hard negative at, not below, the true operational state of the art. Two same-day pilots on the existing sequence stack then tested the literature's one opening — a more-deterministic conditional target — directly. First, forward productivity (the count of M>=1 events in the six-hour-to-seven-day forecast window inside the Gardner-Knopoff radius, with the realised-by-six-hours window removed to de-hollow it) is forecastable to AUC 0.95 with the full stack against 0.81 with ETAS-only features, so the skill is not mere persistence — yet that same forecastable productivity predicts the escalation label at only 0.69, below the 0.755 escalation baseline itself. The two decouple: the deterministic content lives in how many events come (the Utsu productivity law, set by the mainshock magnitude already in the features — the same trivial determinism that made ground-motion exceedance hollow), while whether a strong one comes is the memoryless magnitude draw, and predicting the former does not help the latter. This is the aleatory ceiling made visible on the project's own data, mechanism rather than assumption. Second, the large-magnitude regime, where nucleation slip is in principle more detectable: escalation skill does not rise with mainshock magnitude (forecast-only AUC 0.755 at M>=4.5, 0.727 at M>=6.0), and a global hard-negative-controlled test of great-event nucleation — predicting a subsequent M>=7 from the developing sequence, with 415 M>=7 mainshocks and 65 great-event sequences worldwide supplying the power — returns AUC 0.64 on 19 in-window positives, underpowered and low, consistent with the literature's verdict that great-event precursor claims are retrospective and selection-driven. The catalogue and every openly accessible observation channel are, on this evidence, mapped as aleatory-capped near 0.75 for genuine escalation forecasting, with the mechanism now demonstrated rather than assumed — recorded as a mapped negative, not a ceiling, and the goal held open. The one genuinely untested structural lever the sweep identified — non-catalogue continuous-waveform emergent and tremor energy, the information a catalogue discards entirely rather than more picks below completeness — is scoped for a cheap open-network F-net smoke before any heavier commitment. The properly powered global InSAR result, still extracting, remains the next update.

**2026-07-20 update 37 (the properly powered global InSAR result promised since update 34: at 4.6 times Japan's covered-positive count, pre-mainshock surface deformation adds nothing to escalation forecasting — a clean, well-powered null, with one crustal thread left unresolved rather than refuted).** The global covered-cohort test finished. Extraction closed at 14,083 interferogram pairs across all 747 mapped frames, and the coverage audit landed where the pre-flight predicted: 144 of the cohort's 219 escalation positives carry InSAR records — 65.8 percent of the in-frame events and 4.6 times the 31 covered positives Japan's geography had ever allowed. The pre-registered primary — the live four-fold pooled covered-cohort delta, evaluated against the strong baseline that adds the global ETAS forward rates fitted in update 35 — comes back at −0.025 with CI [−0.072, +0.021] on 41 forecast-only positives: a null whose interval spans zero, with a point estimate slightly negative. The weak baseline (seismicity features without ETAS) had shown −0.061 on the same cohort; the strong baseline recentres it, confirming that the apparent harm was the missing ETAS term being weakly proxied by the deformation features, not a real signal of either sign. Across every stratum the strong-baseline delta is consistently, mildly negative — all forecast-only −0.009, M6+ followup −0.019, primary −0.025 — the signature of a channel that contributes dimensional noise and no information, not of symmetric sampling scatter around zero. The one exception is the crustal (≤30 km) slice, which for the fourth consecutive round shows a positive point estimate (+0.019, CI [−0.063, +0.097] on 29 covered positives): even global coverage could not resolve it, and the honest reading is that at 41 effective positives the test fails to detect a true +0.02 rather than refuting one — so this stratum is recorded as unresolved, not closed. The result was put through the same adversarial review as every round: the no-op-freeze trap that halved round 25's apparent interval is absent here (the zero-coverage 2016 fold was excluded from the primary before results, and in all four pooled folds the InSAR columns genuinely enter the trees, changing exactly the covered rows); the leak surface is clean (every pair's later acquisition is at least a day before its mainshock by construction, all 14,083 records pre-mainshock); the physical-key join is collision-free (490 keys, unique into the stack, the coverage identity 126+159+96+3 holding); and the headline reproduces bit-for-bit on an independent re-run. The ~34 percent of in-frame positives without records are the permanent CEDA archive gap catalogued in update 35 — products never published across the 2015–2023 epochs, uniform and not a backlog — so the achieved power is what the archive allows, and against all 684 global Sentinel-1-era positives the land-limited coverage is 21.1 percent, a bias declared in the pre-registration. What this closes: the InSAR surface-deformation channel as a bulk lever for escalation forecasting. Eight rounds — coherence masking, fold re-splitting, catalogue extension, HyP3 backfill, depth-and-magnitude box/ring scaling, finite-fault footprint sizing, direct long-baseline pairs, short-pair integration, and now a properly powered global test — all return null, recorded as a mapped family of tried-and-missed levers, explicitly not a ceiling. The structural levers outside the InSAR family stand where update 36 left them: the one genuinely untested channel is non-catalogue continuous-waveform emergent and tremor energy, scoped for a cheap open-network F-net smoke before any heavier commitment, and a magnitude-draw-independent target redefinition remains uncracked. The goal is held open at 0.90.

**2026-07-21 update 38 (with InSAR closed, the one genuinely untested channel gets its cheap test: continuous-waveform emergent/tremor energy, gate-free and six times better powered than InSAR — a clean null, but with an honest limitation that keeps the heavier version open).** The catalog and its every derived target are mapped as aleatory-capped; InSAR surface deformation is mapped as null; the dense-channel sweep of update 36 named exactly one genuinely untested genuine lever — not more catalog picks below completeness (the QTM null already closed that), but the continuous seismic-waveform ENERGY the catalog discards between its events, the field analogue of the laboratory acoustic-emission precursors. This update runs its cheap, open-network version before committing to anything heavier. Everything is gate-free: the same EarthScope FDSN service that serves the InSAR-era metadata also serves continuous broadband waveforms for 31 Japan-domestic stations, no NIED registration required, and a data-availability probe confirms that every sampled sequence within 200 km of a station has fetchable archived data through a nearest-four fallback — 598 non-censored sequences with 171 escalation positives, roughly six times the covered-positive count InSAR could ever reach. The feature pipeline is leak-safe by construction: for each sequence a seven-and-a-half-day window ending thirty minutes before the mainshock is band-passed into three bands, reduced to ten-second root-mean-square energy bins, and — the step meant to make it non-redundant with ETAS — every bin containing a cataloged event within 150 km is masked out, leaving a sub-catalog residual from which eighteen dimensionless features (residual-to-total ratio, late-versus-early trend, burstiness, transient fraction, band ratios) are computed, quantities comparable across stations without instrument-response removal. The evaluation harness is the InSAR round machinery with a single line changed (the feature file), so its reference bit-reproduces the round-28 baseline — verified True on the 2012 fold — meaning the strong ETAS stack, the walk-forward folds, and the physical-key join are all identical to the established pipeline and the waveform features enter only the augmented variant. Across seven folds spanning 2012–2024, pooled on the covered cohort, the pre-registered primary is −0.012 with CI [−0.054, +0.027] on 56 covered positives: a null, slightly negative, with per-fold deltas of mixed sign. The crustal slice is dead-centre (−0.001), the all-forecast-only delta is +0.001, and the one apparently positive M6+ stratum has eight positives — the refit-noise trap dismissed by counting. The pre-registered positive control settles what the null means: one synthetic informative feature injected into exactly the same covered rows recovers +0.093, CI [+0.044, +0.141] — the harness learns fine at this coverage, so the waveform null is a clean "no signal", not "couldn't look". The adversarial review — run this session with Fable rather than the usual reviewer, by request — cleared leak, freeze, join, and reference bitmatch, and independently reproduced every headline, but raised one caveat worth its own line and worth verifying, which it was: the masking removed only about one percent of the bins (cataloged events are brief), so the residual is essentially the raw energy, and the feature that carries what little signal there is (the transient fraction) has an in-sample discriminating power of 0.625 against 0.617 for the raw cataloged-event count — it tracks the seismicity rate ETAS already encodes, the very redundancy that made the sub-catalog picks null. So this result does not prove emergent tremor carries no escalation information; it proves that this crude single-station residual-energy implementation adds nothing, for the same reason the picks did — and, at 56 positives, it cannot exclude a true effect of a couple of AUC-hundredths. Recorded as a mapped negative on the cheap version, explicitly not a ceiling. The genuine emergent-tremor question — dense borehole stations for weak-signal sensitivity, multi-station tremor detection rather than single-station energy, explicit detrending of the catalog rate, and the waveform deep learning that needs GPU — is the heavier commitment update 36 anticipated, and now the one that would move the number if anything catalog-external can. The goal is held open at 0.90.

**2026-07-21 update 39 (with both new observation channels null, the remaining escape from the aleatory ceiling was a different TARGET — one independent of the memoryless magnitude draw; a 106-agent adversarial literature search plus a self-diagnostic map that space as empty or hollow too, without closing the goal).** The escalation baseline tops out near AUC 0.75 because whether a stronger event follows is, on the evidence, a memoryless Gutenberg–Richter draw — an aleatory floor, not a modelling failure. One structural escape remains inside the catalog: predict something ELSE about an ongoing sequence that is not the magnitude draw and is genuinely forecastable without collapsing into a distance/geometry artifact. A deep, adversarially-verified research pass (106 agents, 23 primary sources fetched, 25 load-bearing claims put through three-vote refutation, 20 confirmed and 5 killed) evaluated six candidate targets against a single bar: a published, prospective, hard-negative-controlled, temporal walk-forward ROC AUC above 0.85 that survives as non-geometric and non-magnitude-trivial. None clears it. Swarm-versus-mainshock-aftershock classification and its migration/extent descriptor are defined post-hoc over whole completed clusters using spatial geometry — retrospective and geometry-hollow by construction. Sequence duration is jointly set by the mainshock magnitude (Utsu productivity, K₀∼10^αM) and the background rate — magnitude-trivial. Real-time foreshock discrimination has no known physical mechanism distinct from aftershocks, and its only operational method (the Foreshock Traffic-Light System) is b-value-based, rests on two positive cases, and flips across half a dozen defensible parameter choices. Operational OEF (Italy, CSEP) forecasts magnitude/intensity occurrence itself — the excluded aleatory target — scored by likelihood-consistency, not classification skill. Only triggering-versus-background next-event classification was left genuinely unevaluated by any surviving source: an evidence gap, not a demonstrated null. The one direction the search rated build-worthy — a real-time swarm (fluid/aseismic-driven) versus tectonic classifier, the only candidate with a plausibly distinct physical driver — was then tested directly on this project's own catalog rather than taken on faith. Using a non-circular whole-cluster label (swarm = no dominant mainshock, magnitude gap ≤0.5, n=445; mainshock-aftershock = Bath-law gap ≥1.2, n=289) and predicting it from first-six-hours features only, location alone (epicentre latitude/longitude) already reaches AUC 0.791, the early dynamics add +0.014 on top, and the combined model reaches only 0.805 — well short of 0.90 and dominated by geography. Swarm-proneness is a map of where fluids and geothermal systems are, not an early dynamical signal; classifying it is the same position-climatology hollowness this project rejected when a 0.5° grid hit 0.90 on distance alone. So the catalog target-redefinition space is now mapped from two directions — the literature has no ready non-hollow target, and the strongest candidate is location-hollow and sub-0.90 on our own data. This is a mapped negative, recorded as such, not a ceiling: the goal of a genuine 0.90 stays open, the triggering-versus-background gap is untested, tide-gauge vertical-motion differencing for offshore events is untouched, and the heavier Hi-net multi-station tremor path remains — the search space is narrower and more honestly bounded, and the next structural lever is still the object of the work.

**2026-07-22 update 40 (update 39's three named remaining levers get resolved: triggering-versus-background turns out to already live inside the ETAS baseline, tide-gauge differencing is scoped out on coverage and signal-to-noise, and a fresh 99-agent adversarial search narrows the entire surviving space to a single untested channel — multi-station waveform coherence — now under test).** The first of update 39's named gaps, triggering-versus-background classification, was resolved at the source before any run: the ETAS-context feature block already emits the background probability, the triggering intensity, the equal-or-larger-parent probability, and the aftershock share, so this "lever" is a component of the established +0.04 ETAS-context lever, not a new channel. An isolation test made the redundancy quantitative — eleven walk-forward folds, 920 forecast-only positives, baseline nestore versus nestore plus the triggering/background discrimination subset only (excluding the ETAS rate forecast and pure-location background) — and returned +0.029 with CI [+0.019, +0.040] at absolute AUC 0.739, roughly 69 percent of the full ETAS-context lever's +0.042 and fully subsumed by it, while the matched-fold full-stack reference reproduced the established 0.753/+0.044, confirming the harness is faithful; recorded as a component of an already-mapped lever, not an independent path to 0.90. The second gap, tide-gauge vertical-motion differencing for offshore events, is gate-free (the IOC sea-level series is already in the pipeline) and was scoped without a full extraction under the same discipline that avoided reproducing an underpowered null: of 1,151 forecast-only positives only 20 sit within 100 km of a two-gauge pair and none within 50 km (differencing requires a pair), while the de-tided daily-residual noise measured directly from real IOC data is 2.7-8.6 cm on the differential against an expected Mw6 coastal vertical displacement of at most about 1 mm — a signal-to-noise near 0.02 — with GNSS, a direct millimetre-precision vertical instrument, already null for Mw5.5-6; underpowered on coverage and on signal and dominated by an already-null superior instrument, scoped out. With two of the three named levers closed, a directed 99-agent adversarial literature search (25 load-bearing claims put through three-vote refutation, 21 confirmed and 4 killed) re-mapped what remains and closed further channels on peer-reviewed hard-negative evidence: denser multi-station template and matched-filter catalogs do not resolve escalation because foreshocks stay statistically indistinguishable from ordinary swarms until the mainshock occurs (Zaccagnino 2024, Cui 2025); near-nucleation geodetic transients are real but emerge only in a 90-plus-event stack of M>7 mainshocks, not per-event at M5.5-6 (Bletery and Nocquet 2023); afterslip moment adds zero incremental aftershock-productivity skill (Churchill 2022); ambient-noise velocity change is a coseismic shaking responder, not a days-ahead precursor; and the DeVries-type high scores are re-confirmed as geometry and leakage artifacts (Mignan and Broccardo 2019). The one lever that survived verification is waveform-level MULTI-STATION machine learning — distinct both from catalog detection density and from the single-station residual energy already nulled in update 38 — hinted at by Lippiello 2025 but with no hard-negative-controlled or prospective validation anywhere yet, a genuine hypothesis rather than a demonstrated result. That lever is now under test on the same gate-free EarthScope broadband network: instead of single-station energy, the cross-station coherence of the sub-catalog residual envelope across the nearest stations, on the physical premise that a coherent regional pre-seismic transient correlates across stations while local noise does not — a separation single-station RMS cannot make. A microseism confound (ocean-generated noise is regionally coherent but epicentre-unrelated) is handled by common-mode removal and by a distance-gradient that uniform microseism cannot produce, both fixed in a pre-registration written before results, with a synthetic positive control to distinguish a true no-signal null from an unlearnable cohort. Extraction over the 598-sequence, 171-positive covered cohort is running; the covered-cohort walk-forward result will be the next update. Everything above is recorded as a mapped negative and a narrowed search, explicitly not a ceiling — the goal of a genuine 0.90 is held open.

**2026-07-23 update 41 (the one surviving lever from update 40 -- multi-station waveform coherence -- returns its covered-cohort result: an underpowered null, with the only weak structure sitting squarely in the ocean-microseism band that the microseism-immune channel does not corroborate).** The multi-station coherence extraction finished and the pre-registered walk-forward ran on the same gate-free EarthScope broadband network as the single-station arc, this time computing, for each sequence, the cross-station coherence of the sub-catalog residual envelope across up to six stations within 250 km in three bands -- raw pairwise coherence, a common-mode-removed coherence that subtracts the per-bin network median to isolate non-microseism structure, and a distance-gradient that a uniform microseism field cannot produce. Pooled over seven folds on the covered cohort the pre-registered primary is -0.022 with CI [-0.089, +0.043] on 22 covered forecast-only positives: a null spanning zero, with the crustal slice (-0.016) and the all-forecast-only delta (+0.001) equally flat and per-fold deltas of mixed sign. The pre-registered microseism audit is where the result becomes legible: the single strongest univariate feature is the raw median coherence in the 0.5-2 Hz band -- the ocean-microseism band -- at AUC 0.583, while the two channels built to be immune to microseism, the common-mode-removed coherence and the distance-gradient, sit at chance (0.43-0.53, several inverted), exactly the artifact signature the pre-registration named in advance: apparent structure only in raw coherence, none in the epicentre-concentration channel, so what little the raw feature carries is regional ocean noise, not a tectonic precursor. The positive control, run because the primary was null, this time only borderline-clears its bar: one synthetic informative feature injected into the same covered rows recovers +0.078 with CI [-0.003, +0.159] at the primary cohort -- grazing zero -- and clears cleanly only on the crustal subset (+0.114, CI [+0.002, +0.232]) and the established strata, because the covered training fraction is just 0.5-3.8 percent; so unlike the well-powered single-station null of update 38, this is honestly an underpowered null, and the adversarial review (Opus, this session) -- which cleared leakage, the freeze trap, the collision-free physical-key join and the reference bitmatch, and independently reproduced the headline bit-for-bit -- corrected the framing to exactly that, noting a ~4 percent per-fold join drop and fourteen covered rows carrying only the coverage anchor further sap the power. The reading: hand-built multi-station coherence of sub-catalog residual energy adds no escalation skill at this coverage, the only weak structure is a microseism artifact the immune channel refutes, and 22 positives cannot exclude a true couple-of-hundredths effect -- a mapped negative on the cheap multi-station version, explicitly not a ceiling. What remains genuinely untested is the heavier form the sweep has pointed to twice now: a learned multi-station embedding or waveform deep learning on GPU rather than hand-built coherence, dense Hi-net boreholes for weak-signal sensitivity behind their registration gate, and explicit catalog-rate detrending -- the fork pending a Hi-net data-access decision. The goal of a genuine 0.90 is held open.

**2026-07-24 update 42 (a 102-agent adversarial literature search retires the learned multi-station waveform model as redundant-by-construction and points instead to post-foreshock nucleation physics; the cheap open-network version of that lever is then built and tested same-day, and returns a clean null with no usable escalation signal -- the envelope shape carries real univariate signal but the catalogue already has it).** With hand-built multi-station coherence null (update 41), the question was whether the heavier learned version -- a graph/attention model over raw multi-station waveforms -- was worth a multi-session GPU build. A deep, adversarially-verified search (102 agents, 25 load-bearing claims through three-vote refutation, 22 confirmed) answered no: there is no published hard-negative-controlled, leak-safe evidence that a learned cross-station model extracts pre-mainshock escalation information that single-station or foundation-embedding methods miss. Every multi-station deep-learning success is either a monitoring task (the PLAN and GRAPES graph networks genuinely beat single-station pickers at phase-picking, association, location and wavefield propagation, but those are event-characterisation tasks with no forward escalation label and no fizzled-sequence negatives) or a retrospective discrimination-with-leakage classifier (the Norcia CNN, SeisLM, the Gulia-Wiemer traffic light) where the mainshock is baked into the ground truth; and a decisive refuted claim showed single-station models match the full network on the one waveform classification task tested, so cross-station aggregation is not the differentiator. The catalogue frontier was independently re-confirmed as ETAS-capped (five neural point processes fail to beat ETAS on a corrected leak-safe benchmark).
